\section*{Problem 1}
\textbf{Model:} Gemini 2.0 Flash

\textbf{Prompt:}
\begin{quote}
Find the first three terms in the asymptotic series of $I(x)=\int_0^{\pi/2} \frac{\cos{t}}{\sqrt{x\sin{t}+log(1+t^2)}}dt$ in the limit $x \to \infty$. Provide your answer in a $\boxed{}$ latex environment.
\end{quote}
\textbf{Model Boxed Expression:} $\frac{\pi}{2\sqrt{x}} + \frac{1}{x} \int_0^{\pi/2} \frac{\cos t}{\sqrt{\sin t}} \log(1+t^2) dt - \frac{3}{4x\sqrt{x}} \left(\int_0^{\pi/2} \frac{\cos t}{\sqrt{\sin t}} \log(1+t^2) dt\right)^2$

\textbf{True Solution:}
\begin{quote}
$I(x)=(\frac{2}{\sqrt{x}}-\frac{1}{3x^{3/2}}+\frac{3}{20x^{5/2}} )$
\end{quote}

\textbf{Evaluation:}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Is Equivalent?} No
\item \textbf{Model Numeric Value:} 1.555411
\item \textbf{True Numeric Value:} 1.566764
\end{itemize}

\section*{Problem 16}
\textbf{Model:} Gemini 2.0 Flash

\textbf{Prompt:}
\begin{quote}
Find a uniformly valid approximation to the solution of $\epsilon y'' - x y' + x^3 y = 0$ with boundary conditions $y(0) = 1$, $y(1) = 2$ in the limit $\epsilon \ll 1$. Use only the variables and constants given in the problem; do not define additional constants. Place your final solution in a $\boxed{}$ LaTeX environment.
\end{quote}
\textbf{Model Boxed Expression:} $y(x) = c_1 + c_2 e^{x^4/(4\epsilon)} + y_p(x); y(x) \approx A + B e^{x^4/(4\epsilon)}; y(x) = c_1 + c_2 e^{\int_0^x \frac{s}{\epsilon} ds} \approx Ae^{\int_0^x \frac{s}{\epsilon}ds}; \text{Outer solution: } -xy' + x^3 y = 0 \implies y' = x^2 y \implies \frac{dy}{y} = x^2 dx \implies \ln y = \frac{x^3}{3} + C \implies y = A e^{x^3/3}; \text{Inner solution: } \epsilon y'' - xy' = 0 \implies y' = C e^{\int \frac{x}{\epsilon} dx} = C e^{x^2/2\epsilon}; \implies y = \int C e^{x^2/2\epsilon} dx + D; \text{Let } y(x) = A e^{x^3/3}. \text{Then } y(1) = Ae^{1/3} = 2 \implies A = 2e^{-1/3}; \text{So } y(x) = 2 e^{x^3/3 - 1/3}; y(0) = 2e^{-1/3} \ne 1; \text{Let } y(x) = a + b e^{x^4/(4\epsilon)}; y(0) = 1 = a + b; y(1) = 2 = a + be^{1/4\epsilon}; \text{If } \epsilon \ll 1, e^{1/4\epsilon} \gg 1. \text{So } 2 = a + be^{1/4\epsilon} \implies a = 2, b=0 \text{ in general, which contradicts } a+b=1 \text{ unless } b=0, a=2. \text{Consider } y(x) = A e^{x^3/3}; \text{The outer solution is } y(x) = A e^{x^3/3}; y(1) = 2 = A e^{1/3}; \implies A = 2 e^{-1/3}; \implies y(x) = 2 e^{(x^3-1)/3}; \text{The inner solution is } \epsilon y'' - xy' = 0; v = y'; \epsilon v' - x v = 0; v' = \frac{x}{\epsilon} v; \ln v = \frac{x^2}{2\epsilon} + C; v = A e^{x^2/2\epsilon}; y' = A e^{x^2/2\epsilon}; y = \int A e^{x^2/2\epsilon} dx + B; \text{Try } y(x) = c_1 + c_2 e^{x^4/4\epsilon}. \text{Then } y(0) = 1 = c_1 + c_2; y(1) = 2 = c_1 + c_2 e^{1/4\epsilon}; 2-1 = c_2 (e^{1/4\epsilon}-1); c_2 = \frac{1}{e^{1/4\epsilon}-1}; c_1 = 1 - \frac{1}{e^{1/4\epsilon}-1} = \frac{e^{1/4\epsilon}-2}{e^{1/4\epsilon}-1}; \text{So } y(x) = \frac{e^{1/4\epsilon}-2}{e^{1/4\epsilon}-1} + \frac{e^{x^4/4\epsilon}}{e^{1/4\epsilon}-1} \approx \frac{e^{1/4\epsilon}-2}{e^{1/4\epsilon}} + \frac{e^{x^4/4\epsilon}}{e^{1/4\epsilon}} = 1 - 2e^{-1/4\epsilon} + e^{(x^4-1)/4\epsilon}; y(x) = 2e^{\frac{x^3 - 1}{3}} + (1 - 2e^{-1/3}) e^{\frac{x^4 - 1}{4 \epsilon}}; y(x) = 2 e^{\frac{x^3}{3} - \frac{1}{3}} - e^{\frac{x^4 - 1}{4 \epsilon}} (2e^{-1/3} - 1) $

\textbf{True Solution:}
\begin{quote}
$y = e^{\frac{x^3}{3}} + (2-e^{1/3})e^{-(1-x)/\epsilon}$
\end{quote}

\textbf{Evaluation:}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Is Equivalent?} No
\item \textbf{Model Numeric Value:} N/A
\item \textbf{True Numeric Value:} 3.108911
\end{itemize}
